# Language reviews and recordings still needed

Generated by `npx tsx scripts/export-language-reviews.ts --since 4d80b2a`. Regenerate it after the next
batch of lessons lands rather than editing the tables by hand; the sections at the top are written by hand
in the script and say what is waiting on a person.

## What is waiting, and who does it

1. **Estonian, read by the native speaker** (ADR-005 amendment 6). Every Estonian line the lessons and their
   scenes added or changed since `4d80b2a`: 2366 lines (B2 2366). Section 1 below. Mark
   each OK or write the fix; a fix goes into the lesson or scene file the lesson id names, and must still pass
   `npm test` (every word taught before it is shown). For B1 the lines to read first, what each part's author
   was unsure of and what a second model flagged, are in `docs/reviews/b1-flags.md`; for B2 they are in
   `docs/reviews/b2-flags.md`, and the native speaker reads every line of `b2-konekeel` and `b2-kultuur`.
2. **Recordings, by the native speaker.** The lessons are only partly recorded: the catalog in
   `lib/audio/native.ts` reaches lessons 1 to 13 of A1, and a man's part plays the native voice only where every
   line of it is recorded. Section 2 lists 4231 lines and words no recording says yet (A1 681, A2 1015, B1 1357, B2 1178),
   men's parts, model answers and taught words. The known retakes are listed first in that section.
3. **Russian and Ukrainian, read by fluent readers.** Both languages were machine translated, and every
   line was read and corrected on 2026-10-25. A line written or changed after that is not:
   `npx tsx scripts/export-translations.ts --unconfirmed --csv` lists those as a sheet, and
   `npm run apply:translation-review` reads a filled one back. Every line the app holds is in
   `docs/translations/ru.md` and `docs/translations/uk.md`.

Reviews already done are kept, with their verdicts, in `docs/reviews/`: the A1 and A2 Estonian and both
languages' A1 and A2 lines on 2026-10-25, and the same day every remaining written line in Estonian,
Russian and Ukrainian.

## 1. Estonian to read

### B2: How people really talk

| 31 | How it works | ma, mina, pole, ei ole, noh, ju, vaata | Friends talk in short forms and small words: {ma} for {mina}, {pole} for {ei ole}, and {noh}, {ju} and {vaata} to keep the talk moving. Written Estonian keeps the long forms and leaves the small words out. | |
| 32 | How it works | ma, sa, ta, mu, su, pole, mina, tema, ei ole | Talk takes the short forms: {ma}, {sa}, {ta}, {mu}, {su} and {pole}. The long ones, {mina}, {tema} and {ei ole}, are for writing, or for a word you want to stress. | |
| 33 | How it works | Noh, Noh, kuidas läheb?, Vaata, tead, Vaata, ma käisin eile kontserdil! | {Noh} buys a second, or starts a question: {Noh, kuidas läheb?} {Vaata} and {tead} ask somebody to listen: {Vaata, ma käisin eile kontserdil!} | |
| 34 | How it works | Ju, Ta ei käi ju kunagi kontserdil | {Ju} says you both know it, and turns a sentence into a gentle protest: {Ta ei käi ju kunagi kontserdil}, but he never goes to concerts. | |
| 35 | How it works | Nagu, nagu ikka, Muide, täitsa, täitsa veider | {Nagu} is like, the way English uses it: {nagu ikka}, same as always. {Muide} is by the way, for a new subject, and {täitsa} is totally: {täitsa veider}. | |

| 313 | How it works | Pood läheb kinni, Pood minevat kinni | To pass on what you heard without vouching for it, Estonian changes the verb: the [kaudne kõneviis] (-vat, reportedly). {Pood läheb kinni} says the shop is closing; {Pood minevat kinni} says that's what people say. | |
| 314 | How it works | tulema, tulevat, olema, olevat, tahtma, tahtvat | Take the [ma-tegevusnimi] (-ma) and put -vat where -ma was: {tulema}, {tulevat}; {olema}, {olevat}; {tahtma}, {tahtvat}. | |
| 315 | How it works | ta olevat, nad olevat | It stays the same for every person: {ta olevat}, {nad olevat}. Only the subject says who. | |
| 316 | How it works | olevat, Ta olevat müüjaga rääkinud | What somebody did, passed on, is {olevat} with the [nud-kesksõna] (-nud, done): {Ta olevat müüjaga rääkinud}. | |
| 317 | How it works | kuulsin, et, räägitakse, et | Spoken, people just as often say {kuulsin, et} or {räägitakse, et} with the ordinary verb. The -vat form is shorter, and it says you're not the one vouching for it. | |
| 318 | How it works | Pood minevat kinni | English needs a word like apparently. Estonian has only the ending, so {Pood minevat kinni} is easy to read straight past and take as a fact. | |

| 413 | How it works | kas, Kas te tulete?, Ta küsis, kas me tuleme, Mis kell see on?, Ma küsisin, mis kell see on | A question passed on keeps its question word, and takes {kas} where it had none: {Kas te tulete?} becomes {Ta küsis, kas me tuleme}; {Mis kell see on?} becomes {Ma küsisin, mis kell see on}. | |
| 414 | How it works | te tulete, me tuleme | The person changes to who it's about now: Helgi said {te tulete} to you, and you tell Anna {me tuleme}. | |
| 415 | How it works | kas me tuleme | The words keep the order of the question itself, where English turns it round: whether we're coming is {kas me tuleme}. | |
| 416 | How it works | Kas ma saan õigesti aru, et, Kas ma saan õigesti aru, et sa lubasid? | To check you've understood somebody, start with {Kas ma saan õigesti aru, et}: {Kas ma saan õigesti aru, et sa lubasid?} | |
| 417 | How it works | Helgi väitis, et, See on ainult väide | A claim you're not so sure of: {Helgi väitis, et}, she claimed, and {See on ainult väide}, that's only what somebody claims. | |
| 418 | How it works | Kas, Tulete reedel?, ta küsis, kas me tuleme reedel | {Kas} stays even where the question you heard had none: {Tulete reedel?} passed on is {ta küsis, kas me tuleme reedel}. | |
| 419 | How it works, table | Kas te tulete reedel? | She asked whether we're coming on Friday. | |
| 420 | How it works, table | Ta küsis, kas me tuleme reedel. | She asked whether we're coming on Friday. | |
| 421 | How it works, table | Ta küsis, kas ma saaksin aidata. | She asked whether I could help. | |
| 422 | How it works, table | Kes teisele korrusele kolis? | She asked who's moved into the second floor. | |
| 423 | How it works, table | Ta küsis, kes teisele korrusele kolis. | She asked who's moved into the second floor. | |
| 424 | How it works, table | Mis kell see on? | I asked what time it is. | |
| 425 | How it works, table | Ma küsisin, mis kell see on. | I asked what time it is. | |
| 426 | Say it, question | Mida Helgi küsis? | What did Helgi ask? | |
| 427 | Say it, model answer | Ta küsis, kas me tuleme reedel koosolekule. | She asked whether we're coming to the meeting on Friday. | |
| 428 | Say it, question | Ja mida sina küsisid? | And what did you ask? | |
| 429 | Say it, model answer | Ma küsisin, mis kell see on. | I asked what time it is. | |

| 431 | Say it, model answer | See on ainult oletus. | It's only a guess. | |

| 715 | How it works | Me olime pool ööd magamata, et küsida, küsimaks | Two more forms of the -ma word. With -ta on it, it's left undone: {Me olime pool ööd magamata}, we were up half the night. With -ks on it, it's in order to, and it belongs to writing: where people say {et küsida}, a letter can say {küsimaks}. | |
| 716 | How it works | jätma, jääma, jättis söömata, jäi tegemata | The -mata form usually comes after {jätma}, to leave, or {jääma}, to be left: {jättis söömata}, she left it uneaten; {jäi tegemata}, it was left undone. | |
| 717 | How it works | Pool ööd magamata | {Pool ööd magamata} is a phrase every parent of a small child knows. | |

| 719 | How it works | ta ei söönud, ta jättis söömata | -mata is not the no of the verb. She didn't eat is still {ta ei söönud}; {ta jättis söömata} says she left it. | |

### B2: A working week in Estonian

#### B2 25. Will you run the meeting? (`b2-25-run-the-meeting`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1182 | Conversation, Kristi | Tom, kas sul on hetk aega? | Tom, have you got a moment? | |
| 1183 | Conversation, Kristi | Ei midagi halba. Ma tahaksin, et sa juhiksid esmaspäevast meie koosolekuid. | Nothing bad. I'd like you to run our meetings from Monday. | |
| 1184 | Conversation, Tom | Mina? Eesti keeles? | Me? In Estonian? | |
| 1185 | Conversation, Kristi | Just. Su eesti keel on ju juba väga hea. Ja meeskond tahab ka rohkem eesti keelt rääkida. | Exactly. Your Estonian's very good already. And the team wants to speak more Estonian too. | |
| 1186 | Conversation, Tom | Aga ma pole kunagi koosolekut juhtinud. | But I've never run a meeting. | |
| 1187 | Conversation, Kadri | Vabandust, kas ma segan? | Sorry, am I interrupting? | |
| 1188 | Conversation, Kristi | Ei sega. Tule sisse. Tom hakkab meie koosolekuid juhtima. | Not at all. Come in. Tom's going to run our meetings. | |
| 1189 | Conversation, Kadri | Tore! See pole üldse raske. Kõigepealt päevakord, siis räägib igaüks oma tööst. | Great! It isn't hard at all. First the agenda, then everybody talks about their work. | |
| 1190 | Conversation, Tom | Ja kes protokolli kirjutab? | And who writes the minutes? | |
| 1191 | Conversation, Kadri | Mina võin kirjutada. Sina vaatad, et me õigel ajal lõpetame. | I can. You make sure we finish on time. | |
| 1192 | Conversation, Kristi | Ja kui keegi räägib liiga kaua, ütle viisakalt: aitäh, liigume edasi. | And if somebody talks too long, say politely: thanks, let's move on. | |
| 1193 | Conversation, Tom | Hea küll, proovin. Aga esimene koosolek tuleb vist natuke aeglane. | All right, I'll try. But the first meeting will probably be a bit slow. | |
| 1194 | Conversation, Kristi | Pole hullu. Meil pole kiiret. | No problem. We're in no hurry. | |

| 1407 | How it works | arutasime, otsustasime, Minu arvates oleks hea... | The facts go in the past, {arutasime}, {otsustasime}; your view goes in the present or the would form, {Minu arvates oleks hea...}. Mixing them up makes your opinion sound like a decision. | |

| 1672 | Conversation, Anna | Ma ei usu! Töötukassa tahab, et ma maksaksin juuni hüvitise tagasi. | I don't believe it! The unemployment office wants me to pay back the benefit for June. | |
| 1673 | Conversation, Tom | Miks? Sa läksid ju tööle alles juulis. | Why? You only started work in July. | |
| 1674 | Conversation, Anna | Otsuses on kirjas, et ma asusin tööle esimesel juunil. See kuupäev on vale! | The decision says I started work on the first of June. That date is wrong! | |
| 1675 | Conversation, Tom | Keegi on lihtsalt kuu valesti lugenud. | Somebody's just read the month wrong. | |
| 1676 | Conversation, Helgi | Te peate neile kirjutama. Ametlikult, aga mitte liiga ametlikult. | You have to write to them. Officially, but not too officially. | |

| 1678 | Conversation, Helgi | Kõigepealt kirjutad „Lugupeetud” ja nimi, siis kohe asja juurde: „Pöördun Teie poole seoses otsusega...” | First you write 'Dear' and the name, then you come to the matter right away: 'I am writing to you about the decision...' | |

| 1681 | Conversation, Anna | Ja kirja lõpus? | And at the end of the letter? | |
| 1682 | Conversation, Helgi | Lõpus palud: „Palun otsus üle vaadata ja parandada.” Ja muidugi „Lugupidamisega”. | At the end you ask: 'Please review the decision and correct it.' And of course 'Sincerely'. | |
| 1683 | Conversation, Anna | Ametlikult, aga mitte liiga ametlikult. Proovin! | Officially, but not too officially. I'll try! | |

| 1816 | How it works | Ilus ilm, nagu igal jaanipäeval | Irony is said with a straight face. In the pouring rain, {Ilus ilm, nagu igal jaanipäeval} means the weather is awful again, and nobody smiles while saying it. | |

| 1818 | How it works | Kes ..., sel ..., Kes üle lõkke hüppab, sel läheb terve aasta hästi | {Kes ..., sel ...} is whoever ..., they: it's how a belief or a saying is put. {Kes üle lõkke hüppab, sel läheb terve aasta hästi}. | |
| 1819 | How it works | Päriselt või?, Mis siis edasi sai? | {Päriselt või?} and {Mis siis edasi sai?} keep a story going: really? and what happened next? Ask one, and the other person carries on. | |
| 1820 | How it works | Ilus ilm! | Don't put a smile into your voice for irony, the way English often does. Said flatly, {Ilus ilm!} in the rain is the joke; said brightly, it sounds as if you mean it. | |

| 1861 | How it works | juba, ära, olid nad juba ära laulnud, Koor oli juba laulnud, kui me kohale jõudsime | Told aloud, the had-done usually comes with {juba} and {ära}: {olid nad juba ära laulnud}. The whole it's a share of takes the [seestütlev] (-st, out of): {pool inimestest}. And a change says which way and by how much: {väheneb}, {suureneb}, {kahe protsendi võrra}. | |
| 2123 | How it works | umbes pool inimestest, ligikaudu pool inimestest | Said and written are two registers here. Over coffee: {umbes pool inimestest}, about half of people. In the news: {ligikaudu pool inimestest}, approximately half of people. You'll read the second and say the first. | |
| 2124 | How it works | Võrra, kahe protsendi võrra, kaks protsenti rohkem | {Võrra} says by how much, after the [omastav] (2nd form) of the figure: {kahe protsendi võrra}. Without it, {kaks protsenti rohkem} says the same, the way people say it. | |
| 2125 | How it works | osakaal väheneb, arv suureneb, tõus, langus | A figure is an it, so its verb is the third person: {osakaal väheneb}, {arv suureneb}. A rise is {tõus} and a fall is {langus}. | |
| 2126 | How it works | kümme protsenti inimestest | English says ten percent of the people, with of. Estonian puts the whole in the [seestütlev] (-st, out of): {kümme protsenti inimestest}, never the [omastav] (2nd form). | |

Said, it's {küll ... aga} and {samas}: {Vaiksem oleks küll, aga kuidas ma tööle saan?} Written, it's {kuid}, {ent}, {siiski} and {ometi}. | |
| 2160 | How it works | Küll, Vaiksem oleks küll, aga | {Küll} goes after the first word or two of the half you grant, {Vaiksem oleks küll}, and warns that a but is coming. A native hears your {aga} before you say it. | |
| 2161 | How it works | Samas, Plaan on hea. Samas, mis saab poodidest? | {Samas} is at the same time, and between two opinions it says then again: {Plaan on hea. Samas, mis saab poodidest?} | |
| 2162 | How it works | Siiski, ometi, Siiski, ometi, Ent, kuid, aga | {Siiski} and {ometi} both say all the same. {Siiski} is everyday too, {ometi} sounds written. {Ent} and {kuid} are a written {aga}, and they don't start a spoken sentence. | |
| 2163 | How it works | küll, Vaiksem oleks | In English, but carries the whole weight. Leave {küll} out of the first half and it sounds like your whole opinion: {Vaiksem oleks} alone agrees with her. | |

## 2. Recordings to make

### The known retakes

Each of these is a native recording whose text a native speaker would not say in that place, so the text was
left alone. Record the line again with the text the native speaker settles on, change the lesson to match, and
give the new take a new id in `lib/audio/native.ts`.

- `a1-12-saying-no`: Tom answers `Ei, ma ei ela Tallinnas. Ma elan Tartus.` Everywhere else in the course Tom
  lives in Tallinn. For instance, Jaan asks about Tartu and Tom answers `Ei, ma ei ela Tartus. Ma elan Tallinnas.`
- `a1-10a-neighbours`: Jaan's `Jah. Aga minu õpetaja on pärit Lätist. Ta on lätlane.` The `Aga` contrasts
  nothing. For instance `Jah, olen. Minu õpetaja on pärit Lätist. Ta on lätlane.`
- `a1-11-languages`: Tom's `Ma räägin inglise keelt. Ja natuke eesti keelt!` repeats what he has just said a line
  earlier. For instance `Mina räägin inglise keelt.`
- `a1-12-saying-no`, the model answer `Ma ei räägi veel hästi eesti keelt.`: a native puts it `Ma ei räägi eesti
  keelt veel hästi.`
- `a1-10-from`: Tom's `Anna on pärit Ukrainast. Ta on ukrainlane.` says one fact twice; `Anna on pärit Ukrainast.`
  is enough. Optional.
- `a1-04-persons`: Tom says `Ma olen õpilane.`, and later lessons make him an adult with two children. Whether
  `õpilane` is right for an adult on a language course, or `õppija` or a job fits better, is the native
  speaker's call. Lesson 15's `Aga mul on laps!` (not recorded) also gives Tom one child where lesson 16 gives
  him two.
- The `a1-trepil` scene's pick `Ei, ma ei ole eestlane. Ma olen inglane.` reads as a textbook answer; in speech
  `Ei, ma pole eestlane, olen inglane.`
- `a1-09`: the sweep removed Tom's line `Mina elan ka siin!`, so its recording is no longer played anywhere.

### Not recorded yet

Conversation lines are listed only for the men's parts (Tom, Jaan, Peeter), since the recordings are one man's
voice and he never plays a woman's part. Add a recorded line to `NATIVE_CLIPS`, raise `NATIVE_CUT`, and add
Peeter to `NATIVE_PARTS` once his lines are in.
